$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

 
$0.25 \div \dfrac{1}{4} = \boxed{\phantom{5555}}$


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Dividing fractions is the same as multiplying by the reciprocal (inverse) of the second fraction.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.25 \div \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
&= 0.25 \times 4 \\\\
&= 1
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_divide_by_fraction_note
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.25 \div \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
&= 0.25 \times 4 \\\\
&= 1
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$\boxed{\phantom{5555}} \times 24 = 12$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 24 &= 12 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {12} {24} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {12} {12 \times 2} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {\cancel {12}} {\cancel {12} \times 2} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {2}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the missing number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 24 &= 12 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {12} {24} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {12} {12 \times 2} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {\cancel {12}} {\cancel {12} \times 2} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {2}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$72 \div 9 = \boxed{\phantom{5555}} \div 3$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
72 \div 9 &= a \div 3 \\\\
\dfrac {72} {9}  &= \dfrac {a} {3} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {72 \times 3} {9} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {8 \times {9} \times 3} {{9}} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel{9} \times 3} {\cancel{9}} \\\\
a                &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the missing number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
72 \div 9 &= a \div 3 \\\\
\dfrac {72} {9}  &= \dfrac {a} {3} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {72 \times 3} {9} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {8 \times {9} \times 3} {{9}} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel{9} \times 3} {\cancel{9}} \\\\
a                &= 24
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A bus holds $12$ people.

How many buses are needed to transport $175$ students to the athletics stadium?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Buses needed } &= 175 \div 12 \\\\
                      &= 14 \ \text {remainder} \ 7 \\\\
                      &\approx 15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Buses needed } &= 175 \div 12 \\\\
                      &= 14 \ \text {remainder} \ 7 \\\\
                      &\approx 15
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$15$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Mark goes to school from Monday to Friday, but he has trouble getting out 
of bed and arrives late if there is an **‘s’** in the name of the day. 

During the $12$ weeks of term, how many days did he arrive late?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Count of days that have 's'}  &= 3 \\\\
\text {Days he arrive late }        &= 12 \times 3 \\\\
                                    &= 36
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Count of days that have 's'}  &= 3 \\\\
\text {Days he arrive late }        &= 12 \times 3 \\\\
                                    &= 36
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Cormac leaves the house with $\pounds 120$ in his wallet after his birthday.

He spends $25 \%$ of it on a game for his Xbox, and $10 \%$ of the rest on a DVD. 
How much money does he have left?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Money spent on Xbox} &= 25 \% \ \text {of } 120 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {25} {100} \times 120 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {25 \times 120} {100} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {25 \times 30 \times 4} {25 \times 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac { \cancel 25 \times 30 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 25 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                            &= \pounds  30 \\\\
\text {Money spent on DVD}  &= 10 \% \ \text {of } (120 - 90) \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {10} {100} \times 90 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {10 \times 90} {100} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1\cancel{0} \times 9\cancel{0}} {1\cancel{0}\cancel{0}} \\\\
                            &= \pounds 9 \\\\
\text {Money left}          &= 120 - 30 - 9 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 71
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 72$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Money spent on Xbox} &= 25 \% \ \text {of } 120 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {25} {100} \times 120 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {25 \times 120} {100} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {25 \times 30 \times 4} {25 \times 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac { \cancel 25 \times 30 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 25 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                            &= \pounds  30 \\\\
\text {Money spent on DVD}  &= 10 \% \ \text {of } (120 - 90) \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {10} {100} \times 90 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {10 \times 90} {100} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1\cancel{0} \times 9\cancel{0}} {1\cancel{0}\cancel{0}} \\\\
                            &= \pounds 9 \\\\
\text {Money left}          &= 120 - 30 - 9 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 71
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 72$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

On consecutive days you eat $\dfrac{1}{3}$ and $\dfrac{1}{6}$ of a lasagne. 

What fraction of lasagne have you eaten?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of lasagne eaten} &= \dfrac {1} {3} + \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {1 \times 2} {3 \times 2} + \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {2} {6} + \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {2 + 1} {6} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {3} {6} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {3} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {3}} {\cancel {3} \times 2} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {2}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of lasagne eaten} &= \dfrac {1} {3} + \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {1 \times 2} {3 \times 2} + \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {2} {6} + \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {2 + 1} {6} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {3} {6} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {3} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {3}} {\cancel {3} \times 2} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {2}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The sum of two consecutive whole numbers is $91$. 

What is the larger of these two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the first number $= a$

Second number $= a + 1$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 1               &= 91 \\\\
2 a + 1                 &= 91 \\\\
2 a                     &= 91 - 1 \\\\
2 a                     &= 90 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {90} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {45 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {45 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                       &= 45 \\\\
\text {Larger number}   &= a + 1 \\\\
                        &= 45 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 46
\end{aligned}
$ 

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$46$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the first number $= a$
<emptyline>
Second number $= a + 1$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 1               &= 91 \\\\
2 a + 1                 &= 91 \\\\
2 a                     &= 91 - 1 \\\\
2 a                     &= 90 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {90} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {45 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {45 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                       &= 45 \\\\
\text {Larger number}   &= a + 1 \\\\
                        &= 45 + 1 \\\\
                        &= 46
\end{aligned}
$ 
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$46$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

I thought of a number, doubled it and then subtracted $14$. 

I then divided the result by $3$ and got a final answer of $8$. 

What was the original number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(2 \times a - 14) \div 3 &= 8 \\\\
2a - 14          &= 8 \times 3 \\\\
2a - 14          &= 24 \\\\
2a               &= 24 + 14 \\\\
2a               &= 38 \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {38} {2} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {19 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {19 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                &= 19
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$19$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(2 \times a - 14) \div 3 &= 8 \\\\
2a - 14          &= 8 \times 3 \\\\
2a - 14          &= 24 \\\\
2a               &= 24 + 14 \\\\
2a               &= 38 \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {38} {2} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {19 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {19 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                &= 19
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$19$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7:1:11</p>

<li>
ttime
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

A train left London at $11{:}30$ and arrived in Glasgow at $15{:}50$. 

The train only made $3$ stops, each lasting $5 \ \text{minutes}$.

For how many minutes was the train moving on this journey?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time}  &= 15{:}50 - 11{:}30 - 5 \times 3 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
             &= 4 \ \text{hours} \ 20 \ \text{minutes} - 15 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
             &= 4 \ \text{hours} \ 05 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4 \ \text{hours} \ 05 \ \text{minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time}  &= 15{:}50 - 11{:}30 - 5 \times 3 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
             &= 4 \ \text{hours} \ 20 \ \text{minutes} - 15 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
             &= 4 \ \text{hours} \ 05 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4 \ \text{hours} \ 05 \ \text{minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>211A5FCF-4F61-45F5-A728-04F4F08A4EC3</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7:1:12</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g3</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tpercentage
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

The table below shows the results of a survey in which $60$ boys 
were asked what colour of dress girls should wear.

|       |  Blue  |  Pink  |  Black  |
|-------|:------:|:------:|:-------:|
| Total |  $15$  |  $24$  |   $21$  |

What percentage of boys thought *girls* should wear pink?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage}   &= \dfrac {24} {24 + 15 + 21} \times 100 \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {24 \times 100} {60} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 6 \times 10 \times 10} {6 \times 10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {6} \times \cancel {10} \times  {10}} {\cancel {6} \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
                    &= 4 \times 10 \\\\
                    &= 40 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage}   &= \dfrac {24} {24 + 15 + 21} \times 100 \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {24 \times 100} {60} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 6 \times 10 \times 10} {6 \times 10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {6} \times \cancel {10} \times  {10}} {\cancel {6} \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
                    &= 4 \times 10 \\\\
                    &= 40 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$40 \%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A farmer has $364$ ewes and each ewe has either one or two new born lambs. 
If there are $468$ lambs in total, how many of the ewes have twin lambs?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of ewes with twin lambs $= a$

Number of ewes with one born lamb $= 364 - a$

As per the questions,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + (364 - a) \times 1     &= 468 \\\\
2a + 364 - a                        &= 468 \\\\
a + 364                             &= 468 \\\\
a                                   &= 468 - 364 \\\\
a                                   &= 104
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$104$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of ewes with twin lambs $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of ewes with one born lamb $= 364 - a$
<emptyline>
As per the questions,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + (364 - a) \times 1     &= 468 \\\\
2a + 364 - a                        &= 468 \\\\
a + 364                             &= 468 \\\\
a                                   &= 468 - 364 \\\\
a                                   &= 104
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$104$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Arjun gets his hair cut every $2$ weeks. Yahya gets his hair cut every $10$ days.

They both got their hair cut on the same day. How long until this happens again?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

LCM of $14$ and $10 = 70$

After $70$ days, Arjun and Yahya will get their hair cut on same day.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$70 \ \text{days}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>LCM of $14$ and $10 = 70$
<emptyline>
After $70$ days, Arjun and Yahya will get their hair cut on same day.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$70 \ \text{days}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='review-comments'>

<h4>Review Comments</h4>




Let the number of days after they cut hair on same day = n



APTQ,



2 weeks x n = 10 days x n

2 x 7 days x n = 10 days x n

14 x n = 10 x n

14n = 10n



We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Lets find out the value of n which satisfies the condition above.



create a table with four columns try some values with one of them 70.

|  n   |   10n   |   14n   |  10n = 14n  |

..

...



A quick check shows that $70$ meets all the criteria above.



After 70 days, Arjun and Yahya will get their hair cut on same day.



Reviewer: [wf] Date:2022-09-02::21:00



Solve like above as an alternate working.
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Here are the first six terms of a number sequence.

$11, \quad 14, \quad 17, \quad 20, \quad 23, \quad 26$

Which of the following numbers will also be in this sequence?

$30 \quad 40 \quad 50 \quad 60$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by adding $3$ to the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+3)}}  \\
11,  &&    14,  &&  17,  &&   20,  &&  23, && 26
\end{matrix}
$

The next few terms in the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
29, && 32, && 35, && 38, \\\\
41, && 44, && 47, && \green{50}, \\\\
53, && 56, && 59, && 62
\end{matrix}
$

Answer $= 50$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$50$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>
The next term is calculated by adding $3$ to the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:
<emptyline>
$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+3)}}  \\
11,  &&    14,  &&  17,  &&   20,  &&  23, && 26
\end{matrix}
$
<emptyline>
The next few terms in the sequence would be:
<emptyline>
$
\begin{matrix}
29, && 32, && 35, && 38, \\\\
41, && 44, && 47, && \rtq_katex_color_answer{50}, \\\\
53, && 56, && 59, && 62
\end{matrix}
$
<emptyline>
Answer $= 50$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$50$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The rule to get the next term in a sequence is:

**“add the previous two terms together”**

The fourth term is $7$ and the fifth term is $11$.

What are the first two terms of the sequence?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Third term}  &= 11 - 7 \\\\
                    &= 4 \\\\
\text {Second term} &= 7 - 4 \\\\
                    &= 3 \\\\
\text {First term}  &= 4 - 3 \\\\
                    &= 1
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

First $=$ $1$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Second $=$ $3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Third term}  &= 11 - 7 \\\\
                    &= 4 \\\\
\text {Second term} &= 7 - 4 \\\\
                    &= 3 \\\\
\text {First term}  &= 4 - 3 \\\\
                    &= 1
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>First</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Second</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much **water** does she need to make **eight** glasses of squash?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amount of water required}       &= 8 \times 200 - 320 \\\\
                                      &= 1600 - 320 \\\\
                                      &= 1280 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1280 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                                      &= 1600 - 320 \\\\
                                      &= 1280 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much **cordial** does she need to make **three** glasses of squash?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the quantity of cordial needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
320 \ \text{ml} : 8 \ \text{glasses}            &= a \ \text{ml} : 3 \ \text{glasses} \\\\
\dfrac{320} {8}                                 &= \dfrac {a} {3} \\\\
8 \times a                                      &= 320 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {320 \times 3} {8} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 40 \times 3} {8} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 40 \times 3} {\cancel 8} \\\\
a                                               &= 40 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 120 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Cordial needed $= 120 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{glasses}                          &:& \text{cordial} \\\\
= & 8                                       &:& 320 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {320} {8} \\\\
= & 1 \times 3                              &:& \dfrac {320 \times 3} {8} \\\\
= & 3                                       &:& \dfrac {8 \times 40 \times 3} {8} \\\\
= & 3                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 40 \times 3} {\cancel 8} \\\\
= & 3                                       &:& 40 \times 3 \\\\
= & 3                                       &:& 120 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Cordial needed $= 120 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write the following numbers in ascending order:

$\dfrac{5}{6}, \quad 0.5, \quad \dfrac{5}{12}, \quad 75 \%, \quad \dfrac{1}{3}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's convert all the numbers into fractions.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {5} {6}, \quad 0.5, \quad \dfrac {5} {12}, \quad 75 \%, \quad \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5} {6}, \quad \dfrac {5} {10}, \quad \dfrac {5} {12}, \quad \dfrac {75} {100}, \quad \dfrac {1} {3}
\end{aligned}
$

LCM of $6, \ 10, \ 12, \ 100, \ 3 = 300$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {5} {6}, \quad \dfrac {5} {10}, \quad \dfrac {5} {12}, \quad \dfrac {75} {100}, \quad \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 50} {6 \times 50}, \quad \dfrac {5 \times 30} {10 \times 30}, \quad \dfrac {5 \times 25} {12 \times 25} , \quad \dfrac {75 \times 3} {100 \times 3}, \quad \dfrac {1 \times 100} {3 \times 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {250} {300} , \quad \dfrac {150} {300}, \quad \dfrac {125} {300}, \quad \dfrac {225} {300}, \quad \dfrac {100} {300}
\end{aligned}
$

Let's put them in ascending order now.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {100} {300} < \dfrac {125} {300} < \dfrac {150} {300} < \dfrac {225} {300} < \dfrac {250} {300} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {3} < \dfrac {5} {12} < 0.5 < 75 \% < \dfrac {5}{6}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {3}, \quad \dfrac {5} {12}, \quad 0.5, \quad 75 \%, \quad \dfrac {5}{6}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let's convert all the numbers into fractions.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {5} {6}, \quad 0.5, \quad \dfrac {5} {12}, \quad 75 \%, \quad \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5} {6}, \quad \dfrac {5} {10}, \quad \dfrac {5} {12}, \quad \dfrac {75} {100}, \quad \dfrac {1} {3}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
LCM of $6, \ 10, \ 12, \ 100, \ 3 = 300$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {5} {6}, \quad \dfrac {5} {10}, \quad \dfrac {5} {12}, \quad \dfrac {75} {100}, \quad \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 50} {6 \times 50}, \quad \dfrac {5 \times 30} {10 \times 30}, \quad \dfrac {5 \times 25} {12 \times 25} , \quad \dfrac {75 \times 3} {100 \times 3}, \quad \dfrac {1 \times 100} {3 \times 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {250} {300} , \quad \dfrac {150} {300}, \quad \dfrac {125} {300}, \quad \dfrac {225} {300}, \quad \dfrac {100} {300}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's put them in ascending order now.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {100} {300} < \dfrac {125} {300} < \dfrac {150} {300} < \dfrac {225} {300} < \dfrac {250} {300} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {3} < \dfrac {5} {12} < 0.5 < 75 \% < \dfrac {5}{6}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {3}, \quad \dfrac {5} {12}, \quad 0.5, \quad 75 \%, \quad \dfrac {5}{6}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Eleanor receives $\pounds 20$ pocket money each week.
This week, she spent $\pounds 4.88$ on sweets at the shop.
$She then put a third of what was left in her piggy bank.

How much money does she still have to spend?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left with Eleanor}      &= 20 - (4.88 + \dfrac {1} {3} \times (20 - 4.88)) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + \dfrac {1} {3} \times 15.12) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + \dfrac {15.12} {3}) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + 5.04) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + 5.04) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - 9.92 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 10.08
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 10.08$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left with Eleanor}      &= 20 - (4.88 + \dfrac {1} {3} \times (20 - 4.88)) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + \dfrac {1} {3} \times 15.12) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + \dfrac {15.12} {3}) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + 5.04) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + 5.04) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - 9.92 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 10.08
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 10.08$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

Omar is more likely to get a blue marble than a red marble.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {P(red marble)}                           &= \dfrac {4} {4 + 3 + 8 + 1} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {4} {16} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {4} {4 \times 4} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {4}} {4 \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
\text {P(blue marble)}                          &= \dfrac {3} {4 + 3 + 8 + 1} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3} {16}
\end {aligned}
$

The probability of getting a red marble is more than blue marble.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text {False}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {P(red marble)}                           &= \dfrac {4} {4 + 3 + 8 + 1} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {4} {16} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {4} {4 \times 4} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {4}} {4 \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
\text {P(blue marble)}                          &= \dfrac {3} {4 + 3 + 8 + 1} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3} {16}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The probability of getting a red marble is more than blue marble.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\text {False}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

Omar is just as likely to get a yellow marble, or a non-yellow marble.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {P(yellow marble)}                            &= \dfrac {8} {4 + 3 + 8 + 1} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {8} {16} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {8} {2 \times 8} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {8}} {2 \times \cancel {8}} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
\text {P(non-yellow marble)}                        &= \dfrac {4 + 3 + 1} {4 + 3 + 8 + 1} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {8} {16} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {8} {2 \times 8} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {8}} {2 \times \cancel {8}} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end {aligned}
$

The probability of getting a yellow marble is equally likely as getting a non-yellow marble.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text {Equally likely}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {P(yellow marble)}                            &= \dfrac {8} {4 + 3 + 8 + 1} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {8} {16} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {8} {2 \times 8} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {8}} {2 \times \cancel {8}} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
\text {P(non-yellow marble)}                        &= \dfrac {4 + 3 + 1} {4 + 3 + 8 + 1} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {8} {16} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {8} {2 \times 8} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {8}} {2 \times \cancel {8}} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The probability of getting a yellow marble is equally likely as getting a non-yellow marble.
</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\text {Equally likely}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

This time, he wants to draw two marbles of the same colour. 
If he removes them one by one without looking, how many 
marbles will he need to take out to guarantee drawing two marbles 
of the same colour?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of marbles}   &= \text {All three different colour marbles} + \text {Two marbles of highest probability} \\\\ 
                            &= 4 + 3 + 1 + 2 \\\\
                            &= 10
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                            &= 4 + 3 + 1 + 2 \\\\
                            &= 10
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

When Patience asked her Grandfather how old he was, he answered, 

“I am $34$ now and will be $33$ on my birthday.”

She looked confused so he added, “After being $50$ for 
a year I started counting backwards on each birthday.”

How old will he actually be on his birthday?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Grandfather's age}   &= 50 + (50 - 33) \\\\
                            &= 50 + 17 \\\\
                            &= 67 \ \text {years}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$67 \ \text {years}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Grandfather's age}   &= 50 + (50 - 33) \\\\
                            &= 50 + 17 \\\\
                            &= 67 \ \text {years}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$67 \ \text {years}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

A $7 \times 7 \times 7$ cube is painted, and then cut into $1 \times 1 \times 1$ cubes. 

How many of these cubes are painted on exactly two sides?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of cubes painted two sides}                &= (7 \times 7 - 6 \times 6) - 4 + (7 \times 7 - 6 \times 6) - 4 + 5 \times 4 \\\\
                                                        &= 2(49 - 36) - 8 + 20 \\\\
                                                        &= 26 - 8 + 20 \\\\
                                                        &= 38
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$38$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of cubes painted two sides}                &= (7 \times 7 - 6 \times 6) - 4 + (7 \times 7 - 6 \times 6) - 4 + 5 \times 4 \\\\
                                                        &= 2(49 - 36) - 8 + 20 \\\\
                                                        &= 26 - 8 + 20 \\\\
                                                        &= 38
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$38$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>A2AA0292-B690-4FB6-B6F2-07B4E9A2598E</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7:1:29</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tareaperimeter
</li>
<li>
tsimpleword
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Adrian’s garden measures $10 \ \text{m}$ by $6 \ \text{m}$. 

Paving stones measure $0.5 \ \text{m}$ by $0.5 \ \text{m}$.

How many paving stones does he need to pave his garden?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of paving stones} &= \dfrac {\text{Area of the garden}} {\text{Area of a stone}} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {10 \times 6} {0.5 \times 0.5} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {10 \times 6 \times 10 \times 10} {0.5 \times 10 \times 0.5 \times 10} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 2 \times 6 \times 5 \times 2 \times 10} {5 \times 5} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 2 \times 6 \times \cancel {5} \times 2 \times 10} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                               &= 2 \times 6 \times 2 \times 10 \\\\
                               &= 240
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$240$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of paving stones} &= \dfrac {\text{Area of the garden}} {\text{Area of a stone}} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {10 \times 6} {0.5 \times 0.5} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {10 \times 6 \times 10 \times 10} {0.5 \times 10 \times 0.5 \times 10} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 2 \times 6 \times 5 \times 2 \times 10} {5 \times 5} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 2 \times 6 \times \cancel {5} \times 2 \times 10} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                               &= 2 \times 6 \times 2 \times 10 \\\\
                               &= 240
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

&= 50 \times 49 \\\\
&= 2450
\end{aligned}

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

As everyone knows, a normal cat has $18$ claws, $5$ on each 
front leg and $4$ on each back leg.

At Harry’s Care Home for Distressed Cats there are 
$4$ three-legged cats, each one with a different leg missing.

How many claws do they have all together?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Total claws per cat $= 18$

As per the  question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total claws}          &= (18 - 5) + (18 - 5) + (18 - 4) + (18 - 4) \\\\
                            &= 13 + 13 + 14 + 14 \\\\
                            &= 26 + 28 \\\\
                            &= 54
\end{aligned}
$

Total claws of $4$ three-legged cats $= 54$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$54$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Total claws per cat $= 18$
<emptyline>
As per the  question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total claws}          &= (18 - 5) + (18 - 5) + (18 - 4) + (18 - 4) \\\\
                            &= 13 + 13 + 14 + 14 \\\\
                            &= 26 + 28 \\\\
                            &= 54
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total claws of $4$ three-legged cats $= 54$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$54$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A square of side length $3 \ \text{cm}$ is divided into three
rectangles of equal size.

The middle rectangle is removed and replaced on the side of the 
original square to form an octagon as shown.

How many times bigger is the perimeter of the octagon than 
the perimeter of the original square?

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-35-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-35-subquestion-2-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-35-subquestion-2-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{side}$

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of polygon} = \text{Sum of all sides}$


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)



Plot the octagon and label the side length of all edges


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Perimeter of square}      &= 4 \times 3 \\\\
                                &= 12 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Perimeter of octagon}     &= 1 + 4 + 3 + 4 + 1 + 3 + 1 + 3 \\\\
                                &= 20 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Difference}               &= 20 - 12 \\\\
                                &= 8 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

$

</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If the figure shown is folded to form a cube, then three faces meet at every vertex (corner).

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-38-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-38-subquestion-1-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-38-subquestion-1-00.png")

The numbers on the three faces meeting at any vertex can be multiplied together.

What is the largest such product for the vertices of this cube?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Largest number combination $6$, $5$, and $4$ will not come together on a single vertex

Next largest number combination $6$, $5$, and $3$ will come together on a single vertex

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Largest product} &= 6 \times 5 \times 3 \\\\
                       &= 90
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$90$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Largest number combination $6$, $5$, and $4$ will not come together on a single vertex
<emptyline>
Next largest number combination $6$, $5$, and $3$ will come together on a single vertex
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Largest product} &= 6 \times 5 \times 3 \\\\
                       &= 90
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$90$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A square floor is tiled with square tiles and there are $77$ tiles on the diagonals.

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-38-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-38-subquestion-2-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-38-subquestion-2-00.png") 

The diagram shows part of the floor. 

How many tiles are in the room?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the diagram

Let the number of tiles till the middle of diagonal $=a$ 

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of tiles in the diagonal} &= 4 \times a + 1 \\\\
4 \times a + 1                         &= 77 \\\\
4 \times a                             &= 77 - 1 \\\\
4 \times a                             &= 76 \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {76} {4} \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {19 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {19 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                      &= 19
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total tiles on the edges} &= 2 \times a + 1 \\\\
                                &= 2 \times 19 + 1 \\\\
                                &= 39
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of tiles}    &= 39 \times 39 \\\\
                                &= 1521 
\end{aligned}
$



</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1521$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the diagram
<emptyline>
Let the number of tiles till the middle of diagonal $=a$ 
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of tiles in the diagonal} &= 4 \times a + 1 \\\\
4 \times a + 1                         &= 77 \\\\

4 \times a                             &= 76 \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {76} {4} \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {19 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {19 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                      &= 19
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total tiles on the edges} &= 2 \times a + 1 \\\\
                                &= 2 \times 19 + 1 \\\\
                                &= 39
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of tiles}    &= 39 \times 39 \\\\
                                &= 1521 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1521$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
